 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Mechanical sweep of the standard P4 lane (repair-6 item 2).
     3	
     4	Two enumerations, grep-level, no judgement in the extraction step:
     5	  A. every field WRITTEN into a manifest/receipt, and whether its name ever appears
     6	     in a comparison context anywhere in the lane;
     7	  B. every named PASS-gate label, and what the gate's body actually does.
     8	
     9	The point is that the list exists as a checkable artifact. Last round's sweep was a pass I
    10	performed and it missed an item on its own list.
    11	"""
    12	import json, re, subprocess, sys
    13	from pathlib import Path
    14	
    15	ND = Path(__file__).resolve().parent   # re-runnable from any checkout
    16	MODULES = ["p4_lib.py", "p4_evidence.py", "p4_validate_active_lateral.py",
    17	           "p4_build_components.py", "p4_project_4d.py", "p4_adopt_standard.py",
    18	           "p4_check_receipt.py", "p4_lateral_replace.py"]
    19	SHELL = ["run_p4_standard.sh", "run_p4_unfold_std.sh", "run_p4_merge_audit_std.sh"]
    20	
    21	src = {m: (ND / m).read_text() for m in MODULES if (ND / m).exists()}
    22	sh = {s: (ND / s).read_text() for s in SHELL if (ND / s).exists()}
    23	allsrc = "\n".join(src.values()) + "\n" + "\n".join(sh.values())
    24	
    25	# ---------- A. fields written into products ----------
    26	written = {}          # field -> set(files that write it)
    27	# python:  man["x"] = ... / out["x"] = ... / rec["x"] = ... / prov["x"] = ... / "x": value
    28	for f, s in src.items():
    29	    for m in re.finditer(r'\b(?:man|out|rec|prov|foot|ids)\["([a-zA-Z0-9_]+)"\]\s*=', s):
    30	        written.setdefault(m.group(1), set()).add(f)
    31	    # dict literals assigned into those products
    32	    for m in re.finditer(r'"([A-Za-z][a-zA-Z0-9_]{2,})"\s*:', s):
    33	        written.setdefault(m.group(1), set()).add(f)
    34	# shell: printf JSON keys
    35	for f, s in sh.items():
    36	    for m in re.finditer(r'"([a-z][a-zA-Z0-9_]{2,})":"', s):
    37	        written.setdefault(m.group(1), set()).add(f)
    38	
    39	# ---------- is the field ever COMPARED? ----------
    40	# a comparison = the name appears adjacent to ==, !=, <=, >=, or inside require/need with a
    41	# second operand; presence-only = only `in`, `is not None`, or bare truthiness.
    42	def classify(field):
    43	    compared, presence = [], []
    44	    for f, s in {**src, **sh}.items():
    45	        for line in s.splitlines():
    46	            if field not in line:
    47	                continue
    48	            if line.lstrip().startswith("#"):
    49	                continue
    50	            has_cmp = bool(re.search(r'(==|!=|<=|>=|<|>)', line))
    51	            pres_only = bool(re.search(
    52	                rf'(is not None|is None|"{field}" (?:not )?in |\.get\("{field}"\)\s*[,)])', line))
    53	            if has_cmp:
    54	                compared.append((f, line.strip()[:110]))
    55	            elif pres_only:
    56	                presence.append((f, line.strip()[:110]))
    57	    return compared, presence
    58	
    59	IGNORE = {"note", "reason", "title", "help", "type", "default", "action", "required",
    60	          "gates", "result", "endpoints", "merged", "census", "identities", "config",
    61	          "footing", "tree_entries", "band_meta", "sha256"}
    62	
    63	rows = []
    64	for field in sorted(written):
    65	    if field in IGNORE or len(field) < 4:
    66	        continue
    67	    comp, pres = classify(field)
    68	    if comp:
    69	        continue                      # compared somewhere -> not an instance
    70	    rows.append((field, sorted(written[field]), pres[:2]))
    71	
    72	# ---------- B. named PASS-gate labels ----------
    73	gates = []
    74	for f, s in src.items():
    75	    for m in re.finditer(r'gates"\]\.append\("([a-zA-Z0-9_]+)"\)', s):
    76	        gates.append((m.group(1), f))
    77	    for m in re.finditer(r'^def (check_[a-z0-9_]+|require_[a-z0-9_]+|prove_[a-z0-9_]+)',
    78	                         s, re.M):
    79	        gates.append((m.group(1) + "()", f))
    80	
    81	print("=" * 78)
    82	print("A. FIELDS WRITTEN INTO A PRODUCT AND NEVER COMPARED ANYWHERE")
    83	print("=" * 78)
    84	for field, files, pres in rows:
    85	    print(f"\n  {field}")
    86	    print(f"    written by : {', '.join(files)}")
    87	    if pres:
    88	        for f, l in pres:
    89	            print(f"    presence   : {f}: {l}")
    90	    else:
    91	        print("    presence   : (recorded only; no check of any kind)")
    92	print(f"\n  TOTAL: {len(rows)} fields")
    93	
    94	print()
    95	print("=" * 78)
    96	print("B. NAMED GATES / PASS LABELS")
    97	print("=" * 78)
    98	for name, f in sorted(set(gates)):
    99	    print(f"  {name:52s} {f}")
   100	print(f"\n  TOTAL: {len(set(gates))} named gates")

     1	# Standard P4 lane — mechanical inventory of recorded-but-unchecked fields and named gates
     2	
     3	**Why this file exists.** "Records a value, never checks it" and "names a weak check strongly"
     4	have now appeared five times in this chain: `P4_VERIFIER_PASS` (#21), `code_rev`,
     5	`C_syst_eq_retained_plus_active_relerr`, `complete_support_comparison`, and the
     6	full-total-identity overclaim. Repair-5's sweep was **a pass I performed**, and it missed an
     7	item on its own list. So the list is now an artifact produced by a script, checked in, and
     8	re-runnable — not a judgement I assert I made.
     9	
    10	**Generator:** the sweep is grep-level over `p4_lib.py`, `p4_evidence.py`,
    11	`p4_validate_active_lateral.py`, `p4_build_components.py`, `p4_project_4d.py`,
    12	`p4_adopt_standard.py`, `p4_check_receipt.py`, `p4_lateral_replace.py` and the three shell
    13	drivers. **66 fields** written into a product with no same-line comparison, and **22 named
    14	gates**.
    15	
    16	## The sweep's own two failure modes, stated up front
    17	
    18	Both were found by running it, and both matter for reading the table:
    19	
    20	1. **It missed uppercase-initial keys on the first run** — the key-literal regex was
    21	   `"([a-z]...)"`, so `C_syst_eq_retained_plus_active_relerr`, `C_combined_eq_syst_stat_ml_relerr`,
    22	   `M_content_sha256` and `M_shape` were invisible. That is *the exact field the verifier caught
    23	   repair-5 missing*, missed a second time by the tool built to stop missing it. Fixed to
    24	   `[A-Za-z]`; the count went 62 → 66. **A mechanical sweep is only as good as its pattern, and
    25	   the pattern needs its own test.**
    26	2. **It is line-based, so it cannot see a check performed in a loop.** Where a consumer does
    27	   `for k in (...): require(ids[k] <= rtol)`, the field name appears only in the tuple and the
    28	   comparison only in the body. Those show as `SWEEP-FP` below and were hand-verified.
    29

    95	## E-bis. A THIRD pattern, found while fixing the first two: **a gate nothing calls**
    96	
    97	`p4_lib.check_merged_metadata` is a complete, well-tested fail-closed gate — tree completeness,
    98	POT positivity, census counters, native-miss metadata, migration policy — and a repo-wide grep
    99	for callers returns **only its own tests**. `p4_evidence.py` re-implements the same checks
   100	inline with `need()` instead of invoking it.
   101	
   102	So it has been exercised solely by fixtures since it was written. Everything repair-5 and
   103	repair-6 "fixed" in it — the migration-policy comparison, the native-miss comparison — improved
   104	a function **that does not run in production**. That is worth stating plainly rather than
   105	letting the round's diff imply a live repair.
   106	
   107	This is a distinct failure mode from the other two and belongs on the shared list:
   108	
   109	- Pattern A: *records a value, never checks it.*
   110	- Pattern B: *names a weak check strongly.*
   111	- **Pattern C: a gate that is never invoked.** Coverage looks green, the code reads as
   112	  defence-in-depth, and it protects nothing. It is the natural end state of maintaining a
   113	  library gate and an inline copy side by side — the inline copy is what runs, the library copy
   114	  is what gets improved.
   115	
   116	**On BEN-046 vs the code (raised in A+, and correct):** `run_p4_standard.sh:41` still contains
   117	`if [[ -z "${P4_VERIFIER_PASS}" ]]`. BEN-046 is about the *runbook's state table omitting a
   118	standing BLOCK*, not about that gate, and A1 is deliberately OPEN because the token gate is
   119	Joseph's human checkpoint — changing it mid-round would alter the instrument being used to
   120	authorise the round. `329d230` was a renumber and touched only prose. **Neither the ledger row
   121	nor the renumber should be read as a repair of #21.**
   122	
   123	**Marked FIX, but deliberately NOT in this round:** collapsing the duplication means changing
   124	`p4_evidence.py`'s accumulate-`need()` semantics into `require()`-raises, which changes when the
   125	evidence stage stops and what it reports on a bad input. That is a behavioural change worth its
   126	own commit and its own verifier pass, not a rider on this one. A detection test would be: assert
   127	every `check_*`/`require_*` in `p4_lib` has at least one non-test caller.
   128	
   129	## A++. QUEUED — BEN-070 second site, `p4_validate_active_lateral_fps.py:70`
   130	
   131	**Found by the PET lane, verified by Joseph, assigned to this lane, and deliberately NOT started
   132	yet** — it is queued behind the receipts/verifier chain so a shared-library edit cannot collide
   133	with a run in flight.
   134	
   135	- **Defect:** line 70 bounds the diagonal with an absolute `-1e-30` while lines **66/67/68 of
   136	  the same function** are all relative. Same shape as the `p4_lib.py` instance repair-6 fixed.
   137	- **Severity, measured by Joseph, not assumed:** the PSD check at line 68 has an effective
   138	  threshold of ~`1e-89` at this product's scale, against line 70's `1e-30` — it **subsumes the
   139	  diagonal check by ~59 orders**. **Latent, not exploitable. The adopted FPS lateral is fine.**
   140	  This is the same "redundant behind a live relative check" conclusion the `p4_lib` instance
   141	  reached, now independently confirmed on the FPS product.
   142	- **Why my sweep missed it:** the file's git history is the FPS repair rounds, not this lane's,
   143	  so it fell outside the module list the sweep enumerates. **The sweep's scope was drawn by
   144	  provenance rather than by prefix** — a `p4_`-prefixed file that this lane owns was not in a
   145	  list this lane generated. Third failure mode of the sweep, after lowercase-only keys and
   146	  line-based blindness to loops.
   147	- **When and how:** after the verifier pass, and **with a mutation test**, not as a bare
   148	  one-liner — the guard has to be shown to reject what the absolute bound accepts, at the real
   149	  ~1e-86 scale, the same way `BEN044_AbsoluteToleranceAtRealScale` does.
   150	
   151	### The heuristic this third occurrence earns
   152	
   153	Three instances now of **a guard written in different units from its immediate neighbours**:
   154	`p4_lib.check_symmetric_psd` (symmetry and PSD relative, diagonal absolute), this FPS twin
   155	(66/67/68 relative, 70 absolute), and BEN-044's origin (`combine_cstat_bkgsub_100rep.py`, where
   156	`max(max_eig, 1.0)` pinned a relative tolerance to an absolute floor).
   157	
   158	**Adopt the PET lane's detector: read a validator's checks AGAINST EACH OTHER, not just against
   159	the data.** A single absolute threshold is ambiguous — it might be right for its quantity. A
   160	threshold that is absolute while its *siblings in the same function* are relative is a defect on
   161	sight, because the author demonstrably knew the relative idiom and did not apply it there. This
   162	is cheaper and more reliable than reasoning about scale from first principles, and it is
   163	mechanically checkable: flag any `require`/`need` in a function where some thresholds are
   164	normalised and at least one is not.
   165	
   166	**Note it also predicts severity, which is why all three came out "latent".** If the relative
   167	siblings are the *stronger* check, the absolute one is redundant and the finding is hygiene; if
   168	the absolute one is the only check of its kind in the function, it is live. Ask which before
   169	writing the severity down — twice now the first draft overstated it.
   170	
   171	## E-ter. Third mechanical sweep: BEN-035's pipeline exit-status trap
   172	
   173	Generator: `nd-unfolding/tools_p4_sweep_pipeline_rc.py`, over **324 tracked shell files**. Flags
   174	three shapes -- a pipeline through `tail/head/grep/...` used as an `if` condition, the same in a
   175	`&&`/`||` chain, and one followed by `rc=$?`.
   176	
   177	**Result: 22 candidate instances, and every single one is in a file that sets `set -o pipefail`.**
   178	Under pipefail the first failing element propagates, so the shape is benign there. **There are no
   179	live instances in the tracked shell corpus.**
   180

   181	**Which relocates the finding, and is the useful part.** The trap has now bitten five times, and
   182	the most recent -- `selfcheck_receipts.sh` reporting `pass=10 fail=0` beside ten REJECT lines --
   183	was in an **ad-hoc script that was never committed and had no `pipefail`**. All three tracked P4

   185	the scripts nobody commits and nobody sweeps.
   186	
   187	**Rule this earns:** *every* shell script sets `set -o pipefail`, including one-off helpers and
   188	anything written into a scratch directory. A sweep over tracked files cannot see the place this
   189	defect actually lives, which is the same blind spot as A++ (a `p4_`-prefixed file absent from a
   190	list drawn by provenance) -- **twice now a mechanical sweep has been correct about what it looked
   191	at and wrong about what it looked at.**

   193	## E-quater. ATTRIBUTION CORRECTION -- the `code_rev == HEAD` defect was a SPEC defect
   194	
   195	Repair-6b's commit message records the receipt-gate `code_rev == HEAD` flaw as mine. **It was
   196	not.** Joseph specified "bind the token to the sha256 of a PASS receipt whose `code_rev` matches
   197	HEAD"; I implemented that faithfully, and the flaw is in the specification, not the
   198	implementation. He corrected the attribution himself.
   199	
   200	Recording it because **a finding that misidentifies where a defect entered is worth less later**:
   201	the lesson here is about *spec review* -- an equality against a moving repo-wide pointer looks
   202	precise and is a proxy -- not about implementation care, and a future reader tracing "how did this
   203	get in" needs the right answer. The implementation lesson that IS mine is the second one: my
   204	self-check could not fail.
   205	
   206	The same spec flaw was **live in the token gate** (`p4_check_verifier_token.py` rule 4) and fixed
   207	before the verifier pass rather than after, because a push between the PASS and stages 4-6 would
   208	have rejected a valid token and wasted the delegate run.

   210	## F. The pattern is REPO-WIDE, not this lane's — folding in BEN-043 and BEN-044
   211	
   212	Both were found in the **PET lane on the same day**, independently, and both are the same
   213	family this lane keeps hitting. That is the argument for one shared list rather than a
   214	per-lane one: two lanes rediscovering "a gate that cannot fail" in parallel is a repo
   215	property, not a coincidence.
   216	
   217	**`BEN-044` — an absolute tolerance in a ~1e-80-scale problem.** `combine_cstat_bkgsub_100rep.py`
   218	had symmetry tested as `sym_err > 1e-30` absolutely and PSD as
   219	`min_eig >= -1e-9 * max(max_eig, 1.0)`, where `max(..., 1.0)` pins it absolute. Measured
   220	`max|C| = 8.13e-79` — the thresholds sat ~49 and ~68 orders above what they bound, and an
   221	injected asymmetry of half the largest entry left `sym_err = 1.26e-80`, unflagged.
   222	
   223	  **Applied here, and it found one.** `p4_lib.check_symmetric_psd` carried
   224	  `require(... np.all(d >= -1e-30))` — a bare absolute literal against a standard-5D diagonal
   225	  near 1e-79. Now relative (`-psd_atol_ratio * max|C|`), with a reintroduction guard that
   226	  greps the lane for bare literals in `require`/`need` calls.
   227	
   228	  **Severity, stated honestly: redundant, not exploitable.** For a symmetric matrix
   229	  `min(diag) >= min(eigenvalue)`, so any negative diagonal is already a negative eigenvalue and
   230	  the **relative** PSD check immediately above it rejects the same corruption first —
   231	  demonstrated in `test_but_the_relative_PSD_check_already_rejected_that_matrix`. The literal
   232	  could not fire, but it was never the only line of defence. Recording it the other way would
   233	  be exactly the overclaim these rounds keep catching.
   234	
   235	**`BEN-043` — a checkpoint that is not the model that produced the product.** `save_best_only`
   236	plus an `EarlyStopping` that cannot fire (`patience=10`, `epochs=8`) means the in-memory model
   237	at reweight time is the LAST epoch while the file on disk is the BEST epoch; max relative
   238	deviation 0.866 against an aggregate that agreed to 1e-4.
   239	
   240	  **Applied here.** Its rule 1 — *a checkpoint is not provenance unless something asserts it
   241	  reproduces the product* — is the same statement as this lane's legacy-attest defect: a
   242	  receipt is not provenance unless something asserts the producer claim is true. Repair-6
   243	  resolves that the same way BEN-043 implies, by making the claim true by construction
   244	  (re-unfold) rather than by adding a guard over a false one. Its rule 3 — *an aggregate
   245	  cross-check cannot detect a per-event defect* — flags `check_support_comparison`, which
   246	  compares **traces**; a per-bin disagreement that preserves the trace is invisible to it. That
   247	  is a second, independent reason section A marks it FIX.
   248	
   249	**Cross-lane tally of the family, now seven:** `P4_VERIFIER_PASS` (#21) · `code_rev` ·
   250	`C_syst_eq_retained_plus_active_relerr` · `complete_support_comparison` · the
   251	full-total-identity overclaim · BEN-043's unasserted checkpoint · BEN-044's absolute tolerance
   252	(plus its own two cited precedents, CLM-011's `atol=1e-8` against ~1e-38 cross sections and
   253	BEN-042's normalisation mismatch).

   255	## G. BEN id allocation
   256	
   257	**Take new ids from 060 upward.** Two collisions in one day (041, then 044) because both lanes
   258	fetch, both see the same highest id, and both increment. Sequential allocation from a shared
   259	maximum does not work with concurrent lanes. This lane's finding is **BEN-046**; the two
   260	verifier transcripts still say BEN-044 deliberately — rewriting a committed receipt to match a
   261	later renumber would falsify it.
   262	
   263	## H. Standing rule this file establishes
   264	
   265	**A field may be recorded without being checked only if it appears in section C or D with a
   266	reason. Anything else is a defect.** And a gate label may not claim more than its body does —
   267	`complete`, `identity`, `verified`, `proven` are all load-bearing words.

   271	## A+. Contributed by the PET lane, 2026-08-07 — one instance this sweep's patterns cannot see
   272	
   273	Added here rather than kept in a separate list, per Joseph's instruction that the "gates that cannot fail"
   274	inventory be repo-wide and shared. Generator:
   275	`docs/orchestration/audit_gates_that_cannot_fail.py` (624 files, seven detectors, each power-tested against
   276	a reconstruction of the real pre-fix source). Full write-up:
   277	`FINDING-20260807-gates-that-cannot-fail-sweep.md`, ledger **BEN-070**.
   278	
   279	**Why it is here and not in your table already: this sweep and that one look for different classes.** Yours
   280	finds *recorded-but-not-compared* and *strong-name-over-weak-check*. Mine adds *unreachable trigger*
   281	(BEN-043), *scale-blind absolute tolerance* (BEN-044), *size-as-completeness* (BEN-023) and *tautological
   282	datum* (BEN-039). The instance below sits in a file your sweep reads — it is simply not the shape your
   283	patterns match.
   284	
   285	| Field / gate | State | Mark |
   286	|---|---|---|
   287	| **`p4_lib.py:219` diagonal non-negativity** — `require(np.all(np.isfinite(d)) and np.all(d >= -1e-30), "non-finite/negative diagonal")`. Measured on `products/pet/bkgsub/pet_cstat_bkgsub_5d.npz`: diagonal median `3.867e-86`, min `5.510e-102`, max `8.128e-79`. **The `-1e-30` floor is `2.586e+55x` larger than the median**, so a negative variance of `-1e-40` — itself `2.6e+45x` the median magnitude — PASSES. No physically possible negative variance can fail this check. Diagnostic rather than sloppy: the **same function** validates symmetry as `max\|C-C^T\| / max(1e-300, max\|C\|)` and PSD as `ev[0] >= -psd_atol_ratio * abs(ev[-1])`, both correctly relative and one carrying a div-by-zero floor. The idiom was known, used twice, and the third check written in absolute units. Duplicated at `p4_validate_active_lateral_fps.py:70`. | **FIX — new, on no prior list.** One-line: `-1e-30` → a floor relative to `max(abs(d))` or to `abs(ev[-1])`. Left to this lane; a concurrent edit to a shared library during your repair round is the collision CLAUDE.md warns about. | **FIX** |

   289	### A+ reply from the standard lane, 2026-08-07
   290	
   291	**The fix has landed here** — `check_symmetric_psd`'s diagonal bound is now
   292	`-psd_atol_ratio * max|C|`, committed in repair-6 before this contribution was merged. We found
   293	it independently the same day, from BEN-044's rule 1, which is a third convergence. Thank you
   294	for leaving the shared library alone; that was the right call and the collision did not happen.
   295	
   296	**One correction to the severity, and it is the same mistake I made writing it up.** "No
   297	physically possible negative variance can fail this check" is true of *that line in isolation*
   298	and overstates what it means for the function. For a **symmetric** matrix
   299	`min(diag) >= min(eigenvalue)`, so any negative diagonal is already a negative eigenvalue — and
   300	the PSD check **immediately above it**, which you correctly note is relative, rejects the same
   301	corruption first. I wrote a test to prove the hole was live; the test failed, because the
   302	matrix I built to demonstrate it was caught by the PSD gate. So the correct severity is
   303	**redundant, not exploitable**: a dead bound behind a live one. Worth fixing under rule 1, not
   304	worth recording as an open exposure. Demonstrated in
   305	`tests/test_p4_guard_mutations.py::BEN044_AbsoluteToleranceAtRealScale`.
   306	
   307	**The FPS duplicate at `p4_validate_active_lateral_fps.py:70` is deliberately NOT touched by
   308	this lane**, for the reason you gave in reverse: that file is the FPS lane's, its gates are
   309	freshly green, and `CLAUDE.md` is explicit about not mutating across the boundary. Same
   310	redundancy argument should apply there if the surrounding function is the same shape — worth
   311	checking before spending a fix on it.
   312	
   313	**Also, and it affects how A1 reads:** `run_p4_standard.sh:41` still contains
   314	`if [[ -z "${P4_VERIFIER_PASS}" ]]` on `main`. That is consistent with A1 being deliberately OPEN, so this
   315	is confirmation rather than a new finding — but the BEN-046 ledger row reads as resolved while the code is
   316	unchanged, and `329d230` touches only prose. Worth one sentence in the row so a later reader does not take
   317	the renumber for a repair.
   318	
   319	**Convergent lesson, independently.** This file records that the sweep "missed uppercase-initial keys on the
   320	first run … a mechanical sweep is only as good as its pattern, and the pattern needs its own test." Mine
   321	failed the same way three times: two detectors were silent on their own known instances (`\btol\b` cannot
   322	match inside `psd_tol`, because `_` is a word character), the first sweep printed **0 hits** from a `--root`
   323	that had resolved to a directory containing none of the code, and mention-vs-use made the loudest hits the
   324	ledger prose describing these very defects. Two lanes, two sweeps, the same three traps — which is the
   325	strongest argument yet that the pattern-needs-a-test rule belongs in the shared list and not in either
   326	lane's notes.
   327	
   328	**What neither sweep can reach, and where the rest of the family probably lives.** Three historical
   329	instances have no static signature: BEN-032/025 (a check run over a population that cannot exhibit the
   330	defect — a runtime property), BEN-040 (a fail-closed gate that had never returned PASS on real input — needs
   331	execution history), BEN-042 (a normalised quantity compared against an absolute one across two documents).
   332	A coverage harness recording which guards have ever fired in **either** direction on real inputs would find
   333	all three classes at once, and is the higher-yield next step for whichever lane takes it.

/bin/zsh -lc 'nl -ba nd-unfolding/tools_p4_sweep_pipeline_rc.py' in /Users/josephbailey/local-research/MINERvA-OmniFold/.claude/worktrees/gbdt-closeout
 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Mechanical sweep for BEN-035's pipeline exit-status trap, across every tracked shell file.
     3	
     4	The trap: in `cmd | tail -1`, `$?` and the surrounding `if`/`&&` see the exit status of the LAST
     5	element of the pipeline, not of `cmd`. So a failing command piped to tail/head/grep reports
     6	SUCCESS. Five instances now, the most recent of them inside the self-check script written to
     7	catch gates that cannot fail -- which is why this is a re-runnable tool rather than a pass
     8	somebody performed.
     9	
    10	Three shapes are flagged:
    11	  A. `if <pipeline through tail/head/grep> ; then`      -- condition tests the wrong command
    12	  B. `<pipeline> && ...` / `<pipeline> || ...`          -- same, in a boolean chain
    13	  C. `<pipeline>` immediately followed by `rc=$?`       -- captures the wrong status
    14	
    15	Not flagged, and deliberately: a bare pipeline whose status is never consumed, and any pipeline
    16	under `set -o pipefail` where the FIRST failing element propagates. pipefail changes the
    17	semantics, so the report states per-file whether it is set.
    18	"""
    19	import re
    20	import subprocess
    21	import sys
    22	from pathlib import Path
    23	
    24	REPO = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
    25	FILTERS = r"(?:tail|head|grep|cut|sed|awk|wc|sort|uniq|tee|xargs)"
    26	PIPE_TO_FILTER = re.compile(rf"\|\s*{FILTERS}\b")
    27	RC_CAPTURE = re.compile(r"^\s*(?:local\s+)?\w+=\$\?|^\s*rc=\$\?")
    28	
    29	
    30	def tracked_shell_files():
    31	    out = subprocess.check_output(["git", "ls-files", "*.sh"], cwd=REPO, text=True)
    32	    return sorted(p for p in out.splitlines() if p.strip())
    33	
    34	
    35	def scan(rel):
    36	    path = REPO / rel
    37	    try:
    38	        lines = path.read_text(errors="replace").splitlines()
    39	    except Exception:
    40	        return [], False
    41	    pipefail = any(re.search(r"set\s+-o\s+pipefail|set\s+-[a-z]*o[a-z]*\s+pipefail", l)
    42	                   for l in lines if not l.lstrip().startswith("#"))
    43	    hits = []
    44	    for i, line in enumerate(lines, 1):
    45	        if line.lstrip().startswith("#"):
    46	            continue
    47	        if not PIPE_TO_FILTER.search(line):
    48	            # shape C can span two lines: pipeline then rc=$?
    49	            continue
    50	        shape = None
    51	        if re.search(r"^\s*(?:el)?if\b", line) or re.search(r";\s*then\b", line):
    52	            shape = "A: if-condition"
    53	        elif re.search(r"\|\|\s*\S|&&\s*\S", line):
    54	            shape = "B: boolean chain"
    55	        nxt = lines[i] if i < len(lines) else ""
    56	        if RC_CAPTURE.match(nxt):
    57	            shape = (shape + " + C: rc=$?") if shape else "C: rc=$? after pipeline"
    58	        if shape:
    59	            hits.append((i, shape, line.strip()[:96]))
    60	    return hits, pipefail
    61	
    62	
    63	def main():
    64	    files = tracked_shell_files()
    65	    total = 0
    66	    print(f"{'file':<52} {'pipefail':<9} hits")
    67	    print("-" * 96)
    68	    detail = []
    69	    for rel in files:
    70	        hits, pf = scan(rel)
    71	        if hits:
    72	            total += len(hits)
    73	            print(f"{rel:<52} {'yes' if pf else 'NO':<9} {len(hits)}")
    74	            detail.append((rel, pf, hits))
    75	    if not detail:
    76	        print("(no instances found)")
    77	    print()
    78	    for rel, pf, hits in detail:
    79	        print(f"=== {rel}   (pipefail: {'yes' if pf else 'NO'}) ===")
    80	        for ln, shape, text in hits:
    81	            print(f"  {rel}:{ln}  [{shape}]")
    82	            print(f"      {text}")
    83	    print()
    84	    print(f"TOTAL: {total} candidate instance(s) across {len(files)} tracked shell files")
    85	    print("NOTE: `pipefail: yes` means the FIRST failing element propagates, so the shape is")
    86	    print("      usually benign there; `NO` means the trap is live. Verify before editing.")
    87	    return 0
    88	
    89	
    90	if __name__ == "__main__":
    91	    sys.exit(main())

+
+## 2026-08-07 — Re-unfold: NOT byte-identical, but NOT a semantics change. The ROOTs are non-deterministic.
+
+**Job `56471429`**, CONC=6, ten endpoints, 0 failures. The ten 07-18 ROOTs were moved (not
+deleted) to `active_universe_5d/standard/unfolds__SUPERSEDED_20260718/` first.
+
+**sha256: 0 of 10 identical.** That trips the runbook's stop condition, so the chain stopped
+here. But the sha256 test was the wrong instrument, and the right one gives the opposite answer.
+
+**Histogram contents, new vs superseded, all 65856 bins:**
+
+| | |
+|---|---|
+| max absolute bin difference | 6.5e-51 … 5.3e-49 |
+| **max RELATIVE bin difference (worst of ten)** | **1.912e-11** |
+| bins differing | ~10 677–10 694 of the 10 694 reported |
+
+**Coherence test — is it a shift or is it noise?** Pooled over **106 940** reported bins:
+mean `-1.758e-13`, sd `1.961e-12`, so **|mean|/sd = 0.090**; fraction of bins with positive
+deviation **0.4594**. Per band, |mean|/sd ≤ 0.484. The **integrated cross section agrees to
+`-2.6e-14`** — fourteen significant figures.
+
+So the deviation is **scattered and sign-balanced, not coherent**: floating-point
+non-determinism at the 1e-12 level, not a physics change. A real semantics change (a flux
+renormalisation, a different background branch) would appear as a coherent ratio, and does not.
+
+**Cause.** Almost certainly thread-order-dependent summation in LightGBM/OpenMP: the 07-18 run
+used CONC=4, this one CONC=6, so a different core allocation gives a different reduction order,
+and five OmniFold iterations amplify last-bit differences to ~1e-11. **Not confirmed by
+experiment** — confirming it costs one ~30 min single-endpoint run at CONC=4.
+
+**The load-bearing consequence, which is bigger than this comparison.**
+**These ROOTs are not bit-reproducible, so sha256 identity is not a reproducibility property of
+the computation — it pins one particular RUN.** Everything in this lane that treats an endpoint
+hash as a derivation fingerprint inherits that:
+
+- `endpoint_sha256` in `p4_standard_manifest.json` binds an artifact, not a derivation.
+- **The legacy-attest design was built on an assumption that is false.** "Re-unfold and compare
+  hashes" can never succeed, so attestation-by-hash could only ever have certified *the same
+  file*, never *the same computation*. Repair-6 deleted that path for provenance reasons; this
+  is a second, independent reason it had to go.
+- Any future re-unfold changes all ten hashes and every binding computed from them
+  (`endpoint_manifest_hash`, and the merged-inseparability comparison that consumes it).
+
+**What a reproducibility gate must compare here is CONTENTS at a declared tolerance, not bytes.**
+The observed floor is ~2e-11 relative per bin and ~3e-14 on the integral.
+
+Before the run I predicted byte-identity, from J28's remap being the identity on this grid
+(verified, `max abs diff 0.0`) and J33 being a fail-closed guard. That reasoning was right about
+the two code changes and wrong about determinism — the differences do not come from either
+commit. Recorded because the prediction being wrong for a reason unrelated to the hypothesis is
+exactly the kind of near-miss that reads as confirmation later.
+
+**Also, my miss:** the re-unfold script did not use `python3 -u`, so per-endpoint progress was
+invisible for the whole 1h20m (BEN-028; the runbook says to use `-u`). Liveness had to be judged
+from `sstat` CPU time and process count instead, which worked, but the script should carry `-u`.

    Arm a wakerctl watch on the receipts job, and track the notify script it runs
    
    My session died mid-wait once already and took the background poll with it. The job itself is
    in Slurm and survived, but the notification did not -- which is the exact case the CLAUDE.md
    guidance covers: a session-local Monitor dies with the session, a watch does not.
    
    Watch p4-std-receipts-56495756 (slurm-job) now fires notify_p4_receipts.sh, which runs the
    receipt self-check and leaves its output under preflight_logs/ the moment the job reaches a
    terminal state -- so the result exists whether or not anyone is watching.
    
    Deliberately --action command rather than root-resume. Resuming would dispatch into the
    campaign root thread, which is not this lane's to take, and would spend an LLM call on an event
    I am already tracking. The PET lane's nominal-rerun-56445883 watch uses the same shape.
    
    The script is committed rather than left as a stray file on scratch: it is now part of the
    provenance of how the receipts were checked. NOTE for the next cluster pull -- an identical
    untracked copy already exists there (sha256 3e1ec121, verified equal), so the pull will need
    that copy moved aside first or it will abort on "untracked working tree files would be
    overwritten", per the known trap.

+#!/bin/bash
+# Fired by wakerctl when the P4 standard receipts job reaches a Slurm terminal state.
+# Runs the receipt self-check immediately and leaves its output on disk, so the result exists
+# whether or not any session is alive to look. Deliberately `command`, not `root-resume`:
+# resuming would dispatch into the campaign root thread, which is not this lane's to take.
+JOB="${1:-unknown}"
+R=/pscratch/sd/j/josephrb/MINERvA-OmniFold
+OUT=$R/nd-unfolding/active_universe_5d/standard/preflight_logs
+mkdir -p "$OUT"
+STAMP=$(date -u +%Y%m%dT%H%M%SZ)
+LOG=$OUT/selfcheck_autofired_${STAMP}.log
+{
+  echo "=== wakerctl fired for job $JOB at $STAMP ==="
+  sacct -X -j "$JOB" --format=JobID,State,ExitCode,Elapsed -P 2>&1 | head -4
+  echo
+  echo "=== receipts on disk ==="
+  ls "$R/nd-unfolding/active_universe_5d/standard/unfolds"/*.done 2>/dev/null | wc -l
+  echo
+  bash /pscratch/sd/j/josephrb/selfcheck_receipts.sh 2>&1
+} > "$LOG" 2>&1
+echo "self-check written to $LOG"

Cause is reduction order — LightGBM/OpenMP partitioning depends on thread count (07-18 ran CONC=4, the re-run CONC=6) and five OmniFold iterations amplify last-bit differences; `sqrt(N)*eps` for ~1e7 events is `7.0e-13`, the same order as the measured pooled mean `-1.76e-13`. **Why this is a design defect and not a curiosity:** (a) `p4_standard_manifest.json`'s `endpoint_sha256` pins one particular RUN, so it cannot answer "was this produced by the declared computation?"; (b) `endpoint_manifest_hash` is derived from those ten hashes, and the validator's merged-inseparability gate consumes it, so **a correct, authorized re-unfold invalidates the entire chain** — a manifest that breaks on correct behaviour is a defect in the manifest, not in the behaviour; (c) the legacy-attest path (`run_p4_unfold_std.sh`, removed in repair-6) rested on "re-unfold and compare hashes", which **could never have succeeded** — it could only certify that a file had not changed on disk, which is a storage-integrity property, not a provenance one. **What should replace it:** content comparison at a declared tolerance, `p4_lib.check_reproducibility` with `REPRO_RTOL_PER_BIN = 1e-9` and `REPRO_RTOL_INTEGRAL = 1e-12` — a SPECIFICATION set by Joseph 2026-08-07, ~2 orders above the measured floor so a CONC change does not force a re-derivation, with `REPRO_MEASURED_FLOOR` recorded separately so re-measuring can never silently move the gate.     def _get_default_tempdir():
        """Calculate the default directory to use for temporary files.
        This routine should be called exactly once.
    
        We determine whether or not a candidate temp dir is usable by
        trying to create and write to a file in that directory.  If this
        is successful, the test file is deleted.  To prevent denial of
        service, the name of the test file must be randomized."""
    
        namer = _RandomNameSequence()
        dirlist = _candidate_tempdir_list()
    
        for dir in dirlist:
            if dir != _os.curdir:
                dir = _os.path.abspath(dir)
            # Try only a few names per directory.
            for seq in range(100):
                name = next(namer)
                filename = _os.path.join(dir, name)
                try:
                    fd = _os.open(filename, _bin_openflags, 0o600)
                    try:
                        try:
                            _os.write(fd, b'blat')
                        finally:
                            _os.close(fd)
                    finally:
                        _os.unlink(filename)
                    return dir
                except FileExistsError:
                    pass
                except PermissionError:
                    # This exception is thrown when a directory with the chosen name
                    # already exists on windows.
                    if (_os.name == 'nt' and _os.path.isdir(dir) and
                        _os.access(dir, _os.W_OK)):
                        continue
                    break   # no point trying more names in this directory
                except OSError:
                    break   # no point trying more names in this directory
>       raise FileNotFoundError(_errno.ENOENT,
                                "No usable temporary directory found in %s" %
                                dirlist)

    def _get_default_tempdir():
        """Calculate the default directory to use for temporary files.
        This routine should be called exactly once.
    
        We determine whether or not a candidate temp dir is usable by
        trying to create and write to a file in that directory.  If this
        is successful, the test file is deleted.  To prevent denial of
        service, the name of the test file must be randomized."""
    
        namer = _RandomNameSequence()
        dirlist = _candidate_tempdir_list()
    
        for dir in dirlist:
            if dir != _os.curdir:
                dir = _os.path.abspath(dir)
            # Try only a few names per directory.
            for seq in range(100):
                name = next(namer)
                filename = _os.path.join(dir, name)
                try:
                    fd = _os.open(filename, _bin_openflags, 0o600)
                    try:
                        try:
                            _os.write(fd, b'blat')
                        finally:
                            _os.close(fd)
                    finally:
                        _os.unlink(filename)
                    return dir
                except FileExistsError:
                    pass
                except PermissionError:
                    # This exception is thrown when a directory with the chosen name
                    # already exists on windows.
                    if (_os.name == 'nt' and _os.path.isdir(dir) and
                        _os.access(dir, _os.W_OK)):
                        continue
                    break   # no point trying more names in this directory
                except OSError:
                    break   # no point trying more names in this directory
>       raise FileNotFoundError(_errno.ENOENT,
                                "No usable temporary directory found in %s" %
                                dirlist)

    def _get_default_tempdir():
        """Calculate the default directory to use for temporary files.
        This routine should be called exactly once.
    
        We determine whether or not a candidate temp dir is usable by
        trying to create and write to a file in that directory.  If this
        is successful, the test file is deleted.  To prevent denial of
        service, the name of the test file must be randomized."""
    
        namer = _RandomNameSequence()
        dirlist = _candidate_tempdir_list()
    
        for dir in dirlist:
            if dir != _os.curdir:
                dir = _os.path.abspath(dir)
            # Try only a few names per directory.
            for seq in range(100):
                name = next(namer)
                filename = _os.path.join(dir, name)
                try:
                    fd = _os.open(filename, _bin_openflags, 0o600)
                    try:
                        try:
                            _os.write(fd, b'blat')
                        finally:
                            _os.close(fd)
                    finally:
                        _os.unlink(filename)
                    return dir
                except FileExistsError:
                    pass
                except PermissionError:
                    # This exception is thrown when a directory with the chosen name
                    # already exists on windows.
                    if (_os.name == 'nt' and _os.path.isdir(dir) and
                        _os.access(dir, _os.W_OK)):
                        continue
                    break   # no point trying more names in this directory
                except OSError:
                    break   # no point trying more names in this directory

+def require_recompute_hashes(manifest, repo_root="."):
+    """Recompute the sha256 of EVERY referenced artifact path in each endpoint and require it to
+    equal the manifest's stored lowercase-64-hex hash. Never trusts a plausible string: catches a
+    substituted (even same-size) file, a non-hex hash, or a missing path. Login-safe (hashlib only)."""

+def mask_fingerprint(mask_bool):
+    """CANONICAL reported-mask fingerprint: sha256 of the 285-length boolean mask as uint8 bytes
+    (C-order). Construction locked by fps_reported_mask.py against the verifier target
+    REPORTED_MASK_FINGERPRINT (23b2a2f4...). Builders RECOMPUTE this from CV>0 and compare -- they
+    never trust the manifest string alone."""
+    m = np.asarray(mask_bool, dtype=bool)
+    if m.ndim != 1:
+        raise FpsGateError(f"mask_fingerprint: mask must be 1-D, got {m.shape}")
+    return sha256_bytes(m.astype(np.uint8).tobytes())

+def require_reported_mask(mask_bool):
+    """Recompute the reported-mask fingerprint from a boolean mask and require it to equal the
+    canonical value; also require exactly N_REPORTED (266) true bins on the 285-bin grid."""
+    m = np.asarray(mask_bool, dtype=bool)

+def _reject_deferred(manifest):
+    """No field anywhere in a publication manifest may be deferred / partial / placeholder."""
+    def walk(x, path):
+        if isinstance(x, str):
+            for mark in DEFERRED_MARKERS:
+                if mark in x:
+                    raise FpsGateError(f"deferred/partial field at {path}: '{x[:60]}'")
+        elif isinstance(x, dict):
+            for k, v in x.items():
+                walk(v, f"{path}.{k}")
+        elif isinstance(x, list):
+            for i, v in enumerate(x):
+                walk(v, f"{path}[{i}]")
+    walk(manifest, "manifest")
+    return True

+def require_pass_receipt(receipt, manifest_digest):
+    """The MANIFEST PASS receipt (from the publication-manifest builder): schema-versioned, result=PASS,
+    binds the exact manifest digest, timestamped. A bare two-field {result, manifest_sha256} object is

+    if receipt["result"] != "PASS":
+        raise FpsGateError(f"pass receipt result != PASS ({receipt['result']})")
+    if receipt["manifest_sha256"] != manifest_digest:
+        raise FpsGateError("pass receipt manifest_sha256 != manifest digest (does not certify this manifest)")

+def make_transition_receipt(transition, manifest_digest, predecessor_sha, candidate_sha,
+                            central_sha, reported_mask_hash, utc, extra=None):
+    """Build a schema-versioned hash-bound receipt for a covariance transition."""
+    if transition not in TRANSITIONS:
+        raise FpsGateError(f"unknown transition {transition}")
+    r = {"schema": f"{RECEIPT_SCHEMA_PREFIX}{transition}.v1", "result": "PASS",
+         "transition": transition, "manifest_sha256": manifest_digest,
+         "predecessor_sha256": predecessor_sha, "candidate_sha256": candidate_sha,
+         "central_sha256": central_sha, "reported_mask_hash": reported_mask_hash,
+         "validated_utc": utc}
+    if extra:
+        r.update(extra)
+    return r

+def require_transition_receipt(receipt, transition, manifest_digest, predecessor_sha=None):
+    """Require a schema-versioned transition receipt that binds this manifest, the exact predecessor
+    artifact hash, and the canonical mask; PASS; hex candidate hash. Rejects a two-field object, a
+    receipt for a different transition/manifest, or one whose predecessor hash does not match."""
+    if not isinstance(receipt, dict):
+        raise FpsGateError(f"{transition} receipt: not a dict")
+    exp = f"{RECEIPT_SCHEMA_PREFIX}{transition}.v1"
+    if receipt.get("schema") != exp:
+        raise FpsGateError(f"{transition} receipt schema '{receipt.get('schema')}' != {exp}")
+    if receipt.get("result") != "PASS":
+        raise FpsGateError(f"{transition} receipt result != PASS")
+    if receipt.get("manifest_sha256") != manifest_digest:
+        raise FpsGateError(f"{transition} receipt manifest_sha256 mismatch")
+    for k in ("candidate_sha256", "central_sha256", "reported_mask_hash", "validated_utc"):
+        if not receipt.get(k):
+            raise FpsGateError(f"{transition} receipt missing '{k}'")
+    require_hex64(receipt["candidate_sha256"], f"{transition}.candidate_sha256")
+    if receipt.get("reported_mask_hash") != REPORTED_MASK_FINGERPRINT:
+        raise FpsGateError(f"{transition} receipt reported_mask_hash != canonical")
+    if predecessor_sha is not None and receipt.get("predecessor_sha256") != predecessor_sha:

    62	def mat_gates(C, tag):
    63	    r = {"shape": list(C.shape), "all_finite": bool(np.all(np.isfinite(C)))}
    64	    Cs = 0.5 * (C + C.T)
    65	    ev = np.linalg.eigvalsh(Cs)
    66	    r["rel_asymmetry"] = float(np.max(np.abs(C - C.T)) / max(1e-300, np.max(np.abs(C))))
    67	    r["min_over_max_eig"] = float(ev[0] / max(1e-300, abs(ev[-1])))
    68	    r["psd"] = bool(ev[0] >= -1e-12 * abs(ev[-1]))
    69	    d = np.diag(C)
    70	    r["diag_finite_nonneg"] = bool(np.all(np.isfinite(d)) and np.all(d >= -1e-30))
    71	    r["sqrt_trace"] = float(np.sqrt(np.clip(np.trace(Cs), 0, None)))
    72	    r["n_reported"] = int(np.sum(d > 0))
    73	    print(f"[{tag}] shape={r['shape']} finite={r['all_finite']} psd={r['psd']} sqrt_tr={r['sqrt_trace']:.4e}")
    74	    return r

    46	    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    47	    ap.add_argument("--old-combined", required=True, help="old combined cov (support-limited per-band + total)")
    48	    ap.add_argument("--active-bands", required=True, help="active per-band cov ROOT")
    49	    ap.add_argument("--out", required=True, help="CANDIDATE output (never an adopted path)")
    50	    a = ap.parse_args()
    51	
    52	    for bad in _FORBIDDEN:
    53	        P.require(bad not in a.out, f"refusing to write candidate onto adopted/canonical path ({bad})")
    54	
    55	    support, old_total = _load_bands_and_total(a.old_combined)
    56	    active, _ = _load_bands_and_total(a.active_bands)
    57	    P.require(old_total is not None, "old combined total missing")
    58	    P.require_exact_bands(support)
    59	    P.require_exact_bands(active)
    60	    P.component_traces_positive_finite(active)
    61	    for b in P.BANDS:
    62	        P.require(active[b].shape == support[b].shape == old_total.shape,
    63	                  f"shape/mask mismatch for band {b}")
    64	    # exact component sum on the active side (self-consistency of the replacement block)
    65	    active_sum = sum(active[b] for b in P.BANDS)
    66	    support_sum = sum(support[b] for b in P.BANDS)
    67	    new_total = old_total - support_sum + active_sum
    68	    stats = P.check_symmetric_psd(new_total)
    69	
    70	    fo = ROOT.TFile.Open(a.out, "RECREATE")
    71	    n = new_total.shape[0]
    72	    h = ROOT.TH2D("hCov_lateralrepl5d_total", "std pure-component lateral-replaced (CANDIDATE)",
    73	                  n, 0, n, n, 0, n)
    74	    for i in range(n):
    75	        for j in range(n):
    76	            h.SetBinContent(i + 1, j + 1, new_total[i, j])
    77	    h.Write()
    78	    for b in P.BANDS:
    79	        hb = ROOT.TH2D(f"hCov_lateralrepl5d_active_{b}", b, n, 0, n, n, 0, n)
    80	        for i in range(n):
    81	            for j in range(n):
    82	                hb.SetBinContent(i + 1, j + 1, active[b][i, j])
    83	        hb.Write()
    84	    fo.Close()
    85	    print(f"CANDIDATE written {a.out}: sqrt_tr={np.sqrt(max(0,np.trace(new_total))):.4e} "
    86	          f"min/max_eig={stats['min_eig']/max(1e-300,abs(stats['max_eig'])):.2e} (PASS gates)")
    87	    sys.exit(0)
    88	
    89	
    90	if __name__ == "__main__":
    91	    try:
    92	        main()
    93	    except P.P4GateError as e:
    94	        print(f"FAIL-CLOSED :: {e}"); sys.exit(1)
